Honestly, I can't even remember a week since the foreclosure calls started where I didn't do it at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating all that food?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, no, not at all. It's exactly what I meant when I said it feels like a switch just clicks off inside me. I can literally be sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep grabbing the next thing anyway. There's really no control once I take that first bite.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling stressed, anxious, or upset when you eat these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, usually I'm just feeling really overwhelmed. The stress with the house and the calls... it's a lot. So, yeah, I'd say I'm almost always stressed or upset when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd call it guilt. I guess I haven't really noticed feeling ashamed, exactly. Mostly I just feel really, really full and kind of exhausted by the end of it. I don't usually sit there and think about it enough right after to feel bad about myself... I just sort of go to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I've never tried to make myself throw up, and I don't take laxatives or anything. Like I said, I mostly just feel really full and tired, so I usually just go straight to sleep afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely hungry before you start eating those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Most of the time I’m not even hungry when it starts. Especially if I already had dinner with my wife, you know? But I still end up looking for food anyway... it's more like I’m just trying to shut my head off rather than actually being hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your appetite increasing right before you decide to buy and eat those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just mentioned, it's not really about my appetite going up. It's not because I'm getting hungrier... it's really just like I'm trying to shut my head off.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your appetite increases right before these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, it's really not about my appetite at all. It's... yeah, it's just like I'm trying to shut my head off, not because my body is actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating to try to feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's because I'm stressed or upset, to be honest. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because... I've been having this problem with food. After my shifts as a cashier, I keep buying these huge takeout orders. Like, I'm talking enough burgers, fries, chicken, and desserts to feed a whole family. And I'll just sit there and eat almost all of it by myself before I even go home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Once I take that first bite, it's like... it feels like something in me just clicks off. Even while I'm sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you don't need any more,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. I can't seem to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. For the last several months, it's been happening three or four nights most weeks. Honestly, I can't even remember a week since the foreclosure calls started where I didn't do it at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating all that food?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, no, not at all. It's exactly what I meant when I said it feels like a switch just clicks off inside me. I can literally be sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep grabbing the next thing anyway. There's really no control once I take that first bite.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling stressed, anxious, or upset when you eat these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, usually I'm just feeling really overwhelmed. The stress with the house and the calls... it's a lot. So, yeah, I'd say I'm almost always stressed or upset when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd call it guilt. I guess I haven't really noticed feeling ashamed, exactly. Mostly I just feel really, really full and kind of exhausted by the end of it. I don't usually sit there and think about it enough right after to feel bad about myself... I just sort of go to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I've never tried to make myself throw up, and I don't take laxatives or anything. Like I said, I mostly just feel really full and tired, so I usually just go straight to sleep afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely hungry before you start eating those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Most of the time I’m not even hungry when it starts. Especially if I already had dinner with my wife, you know? But I still end up looking for food anyway... it's more like I’m just trying to shut my head off rather than actually being hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your appetite increasing right before you decide to buy and eat those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just mentioned, it's not really about my appetite going up. It's not because I'm getting hungrier... it's really just like I'm trying to shut my head off.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your appetite increases right before these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, it's really not about my appetite at all. It's... yeah, it's just like I'm trying to shut my head off, not because my body is actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating to try to feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's because I'm stressed or upset, to be honest. It's just about getting the food down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed, to be honest. For the last several months, it's been happening three or four nights most weeks. Honestly, I can't even remember a week since the foreclosure calls started where I didn't do it at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating all that food?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, no, not at all. It's exactly what I meant when I said it feels like a switch just clicks off inside me. I can literally be sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep grabbing the next thing anyway. There's really no control once I take that first bite.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling stressed, anxious, or upset when you eat these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, usually I'm just feeling really overwhelmed. The stress with the house and the calls... it's a lot. So, yeah, I'd say I'm almost always stressed or upset when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd call it guilt. I guess I haven't really noticed feeling ashamed, exactly. Mostly I just feel really, really full and kind of exhausted by the end of it. I don't usually sit there and think about it enough right after to feel bad about myself... I just sort of go to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I've never tried to make myself throw up, and I don't take laxatives or anything. Like I said, I mostly just feel really full and tired, so I usually just go straight to sleep afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely hungry before you start eating those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Most of the time I’m not even hungry when it starts. Especially if I already had dinner with my wife, you know? But I still end up looking for food anyway... it's more like I’m just trying to shut my head off rather than actually being hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your appetite increasing right before you decide to buy and eat those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just mentioned, it's not really about my appetite going up. Most of the time I'm not even hungry when it starts. I could have just finished eating dinner with my wife, but I still end up looking for more food anyway. It's not because I'm getting hungrier... it's really just like I'm trying to shut my head off.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your appetite increases right before these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, it's really not about my appetite at all. Most of the time I'm not even hungry when it starts. I could have literally just eaten dinner with my wife, but I still end up looking for food anyway. It's... yeah, it's just like I'm trying to shut my head off, not because my body is actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating to try to feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's because I'm stressed or upset, to be honest. I haven't really noticed a specific feeling like that beforehand. Like I was saying, it's mostly just wanting to quiet my mind and blank out for a bit. I don't know if I'm actually trying to feel better, I just... I just want my head to stop running.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the taste or the act of eating when you're having these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe the first bite or two tastes okay, but after that, I'm not really paying attention to the taste at all. I'm just eating it as fast as I can. It's not like I'm savoring it or enjoying a nice meal. It's just about getting the food down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed, to be honest. I don't really weigh myself very often. My clothes still seem to fit about the same, so... yeah, I wouldn't say there's been any big change that I know of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating large amounts of food are over?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I don't really feel much of anything afterwards, honestly. I just feel kind of numb. Like I said, the whole point is just to blank out, so once it's over, I'm just... done. I don't really sit around beating myself up about it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotional after these episodes of eating large amounts of food are over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's kind of like what I just said about not feeling guilty. I don't get emotional about it at all. I don't cry or get mad or anything... I just feel blank. Once I'm done eating, that's just kind of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating at any point once you've started one of these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, I really can't. It's like... once I take that very first bite, something inside me just clicks off. I'll even be sitting there telling myself, \"Demetrius, stop, you don't need any more,\" but my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. It's like I have no control over it at all until it's all gone.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't really sit around beating myself up about it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotional after these episodes of eating large amounts of food are over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's like... once I take that very first bite, something inside me just clicks off. It's like I have no control over it at all until it's all gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, no, not at all. It's exactly what I meant about that switch flipping. Once I take that first bite and my brain clicks off, I'm not really picking and choosing what I eat. Once I take that first bite, it's like... it feels like something in me just clicks off. Even while I'm sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you don't need any more,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. I can't seem to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. For the last several months, it's been happening three or four nights most weeks. Honestly, I can't even remember a week since the foreclosure calls started where I didn't do it at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating all that food?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, no, not at all. It's exactly what I meant when I said it feels like a switch just clicks off inside me. I can literally be sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep grabbing the next thing anyway. There's really no control once I take that first bite.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling stressed, anxious, or upset when you eat these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, usually I'm just feeling really overwhelmed. The stress with the house and the calls... it's a lot. So, yeah, I'd say I'm almost always stressed or upset when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd call it guilt. I guess I haven't really noticed feeling ashamed, exactly. Mostly I just feel really, really full and kind of exhausted by the end of it. I don't usually sit there and think about it enough right after to feel bad about myself... I just sort of go to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I've never tried to make myself throw up, and I don't take laxatives or anything. Like I said, I mostly just feel really full and tired, so I usually just go straight to sleep afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely hungry before you start eating those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Most of the time I’m not even hungry when it starts. Especially if I already had dinner with my wife, you know? But I still end up looking for food anyway... it's more like I’m just trying to shut my head off rather than actually being hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your appetite increasing right before you decide to buy and eat those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just mentioned, it's not really about my appetite going up. Most of the time I'm not even hungry when it starts. I could have just finished eating dinner with my wife, but I still end up looking for more food anyway. It's not because I'm getting hungrier... it's really just like I'm trying to shut my head off.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your appetite increases right before these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, it's really not about my appetite at all. Most of the time I'm not even hungry when it starts. I could have literally just eaten dinner with my wife, but I still end up looking for food anyway. It's... yeah, it's just like I'm trying to shut my head off, not because my body is actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating to try to feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's because I'm stressed or upset, to be honest. I haven't really noticed a specific feeling like that beforehand. Like I was saying, it's mostly just wanting to quiet my mind and blank out for a bit. I don't know if I'm actually trying to feel better, I just... I just want my head to stop running.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the taste or the act of eating when you're having these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Not really. It's kind of like what I just said about not feeling guilty. I don't get emotional about it at all. I don't cry or get mad or anything... I just feel blank. Once I'm done eating, that's just kind of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating at any point once you've started one of these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, I really can't. Once I take that first bite, it's like... it feels like something in me just clicks off. Even while I'm sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you don't need any more,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. For the last several months, it's been happening three or four nights most weeks. Honestly, I can't even remember a week since the foreclosure calls started where I didn't do it at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating all that food?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, no, not at all. It's exactly what I meant when I said it feels like a switch just clicks off inside me. I can literally be sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep grabbing the next thing anyway. There's really no control once I take that first bite.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling stressed, anxious, or upset when you eat these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, usually I'm just feeling really overwhelmed. The stress with the house and the calls... it's a lot. So, yeah, I'd say I'm almost always stressed or upset when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd call it guilt. I guess I haven't really noticed feeling ashamed, exactly. Mostly I just feel really, really full and kind of exhausted by the end of it. I don't usually sit there and think about it enough right after to feel bad about myself... I just sort of go to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I've never tried to make myself throw up, and I don't take laxatives or anything. Like I said, I mostly just feel really full and tired, so I usually just go straight to sleep afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely hungry before you start eating those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Most of the time I’m not even hungry when it starts. Especially if I already had dinner with my wife, you know? But I still end up looking for food anyway... it's more like I’m just trying to shut my head off rather than actually being hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your appetite increasing right before you decide to buy and eat those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just mentioned, it's not really about my appetite going up. Most of the time I'm not even hungry when it starts. I could have just finished eating dinner with my wife, but I still end up looking for more food anyway. It's not because I'm getting hungrier... it's really just like I'm trying to shut my head off.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your appetite increases right before these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, it's really not about my appetite at all. Most of the time I'm not even hungry when it starts. I could have literally just eaten dinner with my wife, but I still end up looking for food anyway. It's... yeah, it's just like I'm trying to shut my head off, not because my body is actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating to try to feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's because I'm stressed or upset, to be honest. I haven't really noticed a specific feeling like that beforehand. Like I was saying, it's mostly just wanting to quiet my mind and blank out for a bit. I don't know if I'm actually trying to feel better, I just... I just want my head to stop running.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the taste or the act of eating when you're having these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Not really. It's just about getting the food down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed, to be honest. Like I said, the whole point is just to blank out, so once it's over, I'm just... done. I don't really sit around beating myself up about it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotional after these episodes of eating large amounts of food are over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's kind of like what I just said about not feeling guilty. I don't get emotional about it at all. I don't cry or get mad or anything... I just feel blank. Once I'm done eating, that's just kind of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating at any point once you've started one of these large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: No, I really can't.
